I was doing pretty okay before all of this hit.\n\nDoctor: During the past two years, was there ever a stretch of two months or longer when the depression lifted completely?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not two months. It's been pretty much there the whole time.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about many different parts of your life?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean... Before this started a couple of years ago... I mean, I had bad days, sure, but nothing that lasted for two weeks straight or felt like this. I was doing pretty okay before all of this hit.\n\nDoctor: During the past two years, was there ever a stretch of two months or longer when the depression lifted completely?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not two months. Um... like I said earlier, there were maybe a few brief periods where I felt a little lighter or functioned a bit better, but it never completely went away for that long. It's been pretty much there the whole time.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about many different parts of your life?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean... I have normal stress, but I wouldn't say I worry excessively about a bunch of different things. It's more just the... the feeling down and the lack of energy that takes over, rather than feeling anxious or worried all the time.\n\nDoctor: During these past two years, was there ever a time when you felt completely free of depression for at least two months?\n\nPatient: Um, I think you might have just asked me that one... but no. Like I said a minute ago, I've never had a two-month break from it. It hasn't ever fully gone away for that long.\n\nDoctor: During this two-year period, was there ever a time when your depression became clearly worse than usual for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure if it was exactly two weeks, but... yeah, there are definitely times when it gets a lot heavier. Like, the baseline is always kind of low, but sometimes it just dips even further and everything feels a lot harder to do. I don't know if I ever tracked exactly how long those worse periods lasted, though.\n\nDoctor: During one of those times when your depression got worse, was there ever a stretch of at least two weeks when you had five or more depression symptoms most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure. I mean, I know it gets worse sometimes, but I haven't really sat down and counted up the symptoms or tracked if it was exactly five of them for two weeks straight. It's just... it just feels heavier, you know? But I can't really give you a specific number of symptoms like that.\n\nDoctor: During the times when your depression feels worse, do you have trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do. Um, most nights I just lie awake for hours, listening to the house settle. And then, even if I do fall asleep, I usually wake up around three or four in the morning. After that, I just can't get back to sleep before I have to get up for work.\n\nDoctor: During those times when your depression gets worse, do you notice a clear drop in your appetite?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my appetite gets pretty messed up. Um, it's all over the place, really. Mostly, I just end up skipping actual meals during the day. I'll just sort of live on coffee and toast, or whatever is easiest to grab. I don't always notice it right away, but then I'll realize my clothes are fitting a lot looser than they used to.\n\nDoctor: During those times when your depression gets worse, do you have trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed that, no. I mean, work can be stressful and sometimes I'm just tired from not sleeping, but I wouldn't say I have a hard time focusing on things or getting my work done. It's more just the physical tiredness than my brain not working right.\n\nDoctor: During the times when your depression gets worse, do you feel unusually tired or low on energy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I'm tired because I'm not sleeping well, like I said, but it's not like I can't get out of bed or do my job. I still have enough energy to get through the day and do what I need to do. It's just normal tiredness from being awake half the night, not some deep exhaustion.\n\nDoctor: During those times when your depression gets worse, do you move or speak noticeably more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, I do.
